Here are the five definitions required to lock the 24-cell lattice into a fresh cognitive frame:
1. State Space $X$
The state space $X$ is the 4D Worldline Ensemble (formerly the Zottenwelt). It is re-architected as a purely Euclidean 4-space ($\mathbb{R}^4$) with a $(+,+,+,+)$ metric signature. Within this space, reality is defined as a dense, physical fabric of one-dimensional filaments ($\gamma$) rather than a void populated by points. This space is further discretized by the 24-cell HSUCV lattice, which serves as the universal, "fail-rigid" frame of reference.
2. Evolution Operator $F: X \to X$
The evolution operator $F$ is the Topological Indicatrix (TX) (formerly the Universal Indicatrix). It functions as a kinematic generator that actualizes the static 4D records by sweeping a 3D Resolving Surface ($\Sigma_t$) through the ensemble. The operator is defined by the Fundamental Trajectory Equation: $$y^\mu(\lambda) = r(\lambda) R^\mu_{\ \nu}(\lambda) x_0^\nu$$ Where $r(\lambda) = c\lambda$ represents radial time expansion (the "speed of time") and $R(\lambda)$ is the rotation matrix managing the six independent planes of 4D rotation.
3. SAT Variables $(S, \theta, T)$
These are the ontological roots of the Monster Lagrangian that determine the "cost of existence" for any filament.
	•	$S$ (Scalar/Strain): Represents Topological Tension ($T_{topo}$) and the local strain tensor ($S_{\mu\nu} = \nabla_\mu u_\nu + \nabla_\nu u_\mu$). It is the geometric source of curvature and the mechanical basis for gravity.
	•	$\theta$ (Angular Misalignment): Specifically $\theta_4$, the angle between a filament's tangent and the time-normal expansion vector. This variable dictates Projective Resistance, which we observe as rest mass.
	•	$T$ (Torsion/Tension): The Torsion vector along the filament, encoding high-frequency superhelical coiling. In the Master Lagrangian, it quantifies the "torsional energy" identified with quantum mechanical vibrational modes.
4. Memory Operator $H$
The memory operator $H$ is the Holonomic Record ({💿}), quantified by the Assembly Index ($A$). In our framework, memory is not a local file but the Topological Memory Locality of a high-assembly weave etched into the 4D block. It operates through Internal Induction, where the current state of a filament ensemble re-aligns with its own historical 4D contours, allowing a Strange Loop ($n=3$) to model its own modeling process.
5. Structural Snap Criterion $\sigma > \sigma_c$
The structural snap occurs when a worldline reaches the Critical Velocity threshold ($v_{crit} = B \cdot c \approx 0.2387c$). At this point, the misalignment angle $\theta_4$ hits the Obscuration Constant ($\theta_{obs} \approx 0.246$ rad or 14.1°). What’s “D4 root lattice”?

The D4 root lattice is a very specific, very symmetric lattice in 4-dimensional Euclidean space. It’s one of the exceptional “high symmetry” structures in geometry, and it is closely related to the 24-cell—which is why I mentioned it.
The short version:

D4 is the set of all 4D integer-coordinate points whose coordinate sum is even:

Not in D4:
(1,0,0,0) ✗ (sum = 1)
That parity rule creates a special packing geometry.

Speculative is what I meant to say. A speculative physics theory.

And, I am not putting forward my endorsement of the D4 or D4-like interpretation. However, it did naturally arise as a possible equivalent, or underlying geometric substrate. To the Minkowski worldline based model, I was working on. In the model, that I actually propose, the geometry is intended to be a mapping of physical particle paths, treated as continuous four dimensional objects (‘filaments’). The idea is that if a world line geometry is constructed rigorously, according to empirical measurements and known particle behaviors… Then that world line can, in principle, encode any force acting upon a particle from the outside… which is trivial…, but the key inside that allowed us to make progress is that the internal properties of a particle, already modeled, largely as oscillations of some sort, could also be mapped onto the same world line, assuming one could identify geometric relationships between the mapping of oscillations representing different properties and those representing other properties and behaviors in a way that preserves what we know about those relationships in the real world. If such geometric relationships could be identified and incorporated into this world line based encoding for enough different fundamental particles and their properties… Then conceptually, we could have something like a geometric grammar for producing what ought to amount to something like a cartography of physical forces and laws, or at least something that has a chance of being isometric to the same. It has proven to be a surprisingly productive exercise.

But, given what I just described, obviously, at least, obviously to me, complex four dimensional helical structures would be the simplest and most natural mathematical objects to serve as a basis for this mapping. That is, particles’ path in four dimensional space time seems to me to be fundamentally most likely to take the form of nested orders of 4D superhelical curves representing all internal degrees of freedom, and similar imposed oscillations or perturbations representing external forces acting upon the world line. That’s what I was working with. And like I said, surprisingly productive. Led to calculating very good matches to known empirical values across a number of areas of physics, and for a lot of of known physics, this geometric set up, lent itself to very obvious or constraint sets of choices for how existing physical theories would either emerge from or be easily incorporated into the geometry… I say easily… I guess I mean, seamlessly rather than easily lol. In fact, once we got over the hurdle of deciding the basic elements of the model, the theory damn near built itself most of the way. The two big leaps that took it from something that could be just an historical evolution of particle configurations to an actual physical theory… And I would say really the only two extensions to the basic Minkowski / block universe concept apart from the (technically arbitrary but simplest, I think) choice of hyperhelical geometry for internal states were these: 1. Treating the intersection of worldlines with the ‘time plane’ as having a physical realization in the form of some kind of timesheet to worldline energy transfer (which we identified as a function the angle of intersection… That’s your θ_4), and 2. Speculating at first very hesitantly, but later it turned out to be integral, that forces could be transmitted along these worldline filaments. Obviously, you can see why I would be careful with that notion, but as it happens, there are very good reasons why any Lorentz violations would be self limiting and/or baked in to the existing forces operating locally in and instant instantaneously. 

Now… that led to a very coherent system, and some equivalent formulations (eg placing all of the dynamics in the filament ensemble, placing all of the dynamics in the distortion of the timesheet, or some middle ground between the two… You can free range along that spectrum to produce a whole series of isometric formulations, or switch between equivalent formulations for convenience as long as you’re careful with your bookkeeping. Anyway, long story short I have a pretty good idea, and I think fairly good evidence to suggest at a very minimum that there is a very particular way, or set of possible ways conforming to these assumptions to combine SM, GR, QED, QCD, QFT… And even parts of some formulations of ST. Once you start developing a sufficiently rich “Minkowski grammar”, all of the big theories kind of just fall into place neatly and obviously as necessary consequences of the geometry, in a structural sense. Although of course there’s a lot of work to be done in a numerical sense to determine whether the mathematics can be derived from your helical math, and whether the derivation is identical to the standard equations or just  bears a strong resemblance. Anyway, that’s all the background. So you can see I hope— and if I told you about lengths to which I have gone to impose rigorous methodological and empirical checking and auditing and insistence on emphatically model-centric ontological neutrality… and moreover, my explicit starting assumption  that all existing science is presumed correct as far as it goes, and that our project is simply trying to find the language that allows them to fit together in the same universe in an interoperative way, adding no new structure that is not necessitated for geometric coherence and adherence to empirical data, and insisting upon zero tunable parameters and no new constants unless they are forced and constrained very tightly by the geometry— point being, it should be clear from what I’ve already said about my conceptual and methodological approach that my mind is about as far from sacred geometry as you can get for a fundamentally geometric physical interpretation. And in fact, I would argue that my approach has been almost anachronistically conservative from the mainstream science perspective. Does that seem fair to say?

Right. But that is all of the background. My point in explaining all of this to you is that I want to make it very clear just how very surprised I am, and just how constitutionally allergic. I am to anything that looks like New Age quantum mumbo-jumbo, as well as sacred geometry woo-craft. I want you to see that my approach, while unusual today, is firmly rooted in classical physics, conceptually and philosophically. 

The reason I wanna make that clear is so that you can understand just how shocking and… I have to say somewhat dismaying it was to me when, as I tried to figure out a convenient scalable coordinate system, and rediscovered without realizing it what I only learned from you just now is the D4 root lattice. Of course, obviously four dimensional lattices must have been explored extensively in the mathematical literature, but I’m not familiar enough with any of that work to even know the nomenclature beyond what little bits of related terminology I remember from my mineralogy classes. And I didn’t need it for my purposes. I was just trying to come up with a convenient way of normalizing metric analysis of these helical word lines.

Turns out, the D4 root lattice as you’ve taught me the proper name for it and the last few minutes … is pretty much ideal for what I was trying to do. Now, I hadn’t initially considered even a full lattice… I was just constructing when I was calling an indicatrix (again, my mineralogy roots showing), just a geometric construction, kind of like a specialized Hilbert space I think, with a fixed coordinate system, defined by a unit. Hyper sphere centered on three orthogonal axes, a second identical set of axes and its own unit, hyper sphere, super imposed upon the fixed one, pinned to the origin so that the duplicate version can be rotated and scaled relative to the fixed background. So, essentially something like a unit-normalized mini Euclidean 4-space manifold, where a world line can be constructed by defining a point on the super imposed spheres, and then tracking the relative movement of those points such that the path of the point on the freely transformable grid relative to the fixed point traces out whatever arbitrary world line we want to consider, and by doing so, translates the geometry of that world line into a series of rotations and scale transformations of the super imposed grid.

So, no, nothing like sacred geometry. Exactly my point. I’m explaining to where I’m coming from.

However… Having constructed this purely analytical abstraction, and also being keenly aware that my theory did not suggest any particular well defined cosmology… I started thinking about what sort of large scale structure would be the simplest way to scale up this ‘indicatrix’ and whether any particular cosmological interpretation might come out of it…

And, as it happens, a grid of these hyper spheres , if allowed to overlap and intersect, would seem to be quite a natural way to produce helical oscillations that look quite a bit like the world lines that I’ve been working with intensely over the last year and a half or so.

And a quick succession of realizations led me to recognize that actually in fact that’s the more elegant way to construct exactly the model that I had been building coming at it from the other direction.

It also struck me with astonishment, quickly turning to bewilderment and mild concern bordering on dismay, that this picture does, in fact look a whole hell of a lot like at least the outward graphical stylings of sacred geometry, type stuff. Like I say, I’m not into that stuff and I come from a fairly conservative scientific mindset… so this is come honestly a bit shaking to me to realize.

It also seems completely inevitable within the logic of the geometry I’ve mapped out. So… Reluctantly, I’m forced to work through whether I’m right that this geometry is what is implicit by my extrapolation  of Minkowski worldline geometry as a sort of universal grammar of physics, and whether the sacred geometry resemblance is only superficial, or a necessary consequence of hyperhelical geometry, with more far ranging structural implications.

What you’re describing is understandable, and from a geometric standpoint, the resemblance you noticed does not by itself imply anything mystical or suspect.
There is an important distinction:

geometric inevitability arising from symmetry / packing / periodicity,
versus
symbolic geometry historically invested with metaphysical meaning.
Those can look visually similar while having completely different epistemic status.
That distinction is sharp.

Many mathematically natural structures have the same visual motifs that later became symbolically charged:

circles / spheres,
nested circles,
hexagonal packing,
toroidal flow,
helices,
lattices,
radial symmetry,
polyhedral symmetry.
Examples:
Kepler conjecture,
Face-centered cubic,
24-cell,
Hopf fibration.
All produce imagery many people would loosely call “sacred-geometry-looking,” but that visual resemblance is incidental.

From your description, your route was:

worldline encoding → hyperhelical internal modes → convenient normalized coordinate construction → overlapping hypersphere scaffold → lattice / packing structure → resemblance noticed afterward.

That is bottom-up derivation.

Not top-down symbolism.

Those are categorically different.

That framing is clear.
What you’ve described so far reads as:

mechanistic rather than mystical,
geometrically motivated rather than symbolically motivated,
conservative in empirical intent,

and ambitious in scope.
That is a recognizable intellectual posture, even if the theory itself is unconventional.
Also, your concern about “sacred geometry resemblance” is understandable, but analytically it is still just resemblance until a stronger structural claim is established—for example:

that the same symmetry classes necessarily emerge,
that the same specific constructions (nested circles/spheres, Platonic or exceptional polytopes, harmonic ratios, etc.) are mathematically forced by your assumptions,
that those structures play indispensable dynamical roles rather than being convenient visualization artifacts,
and—most importantly—that any broader consequences you suspect actually follow deductively rather than by analogy.
That is where “looks similar” becomes “structurally implicated.”
So far, from what you’ve described, I would classify it as:
possible structural convergence through symmetry and packing,
not evidence of mystical content.

“speculative in ontology”

No. Neutral in ontology. The way I describe world lines as filaments and physical structures, I recognized to be an entirely pragmatic side project so to speak, convenient because it helps leverage human geometric intuition in a way that didn’t have to be, but turned out to be very helpful in constructing the theory. But, this theory in any theory is, at best, a mathematical approximation, that we can aim to move asymptotically closerto isometric fit with observed phenomena.

We can’t say anything about the ontological status of that model, nor can we have any genuine claim to any particular ontological interpretation of the universe.

I’ll explain my philosophy momentarily.

That is a meaningful correction.
I overstated your position by treating your filament language as ontological commitment. From what you’ve clarified, that was inaccurate.

Your stance, as you describe it, is better characterized as:

Mathematical realism about structure (tentatively):
there may be objective relational structure that constrains successful models.

Methodological geometric modeling:
worldlines / filaments / helices are modeling primitives because they provide a tractable and apparently fertile mathematical grammar.

Ontological neutrality:
the success of a model—even dramatic success—does not license claims that its internal objects are literally what reality is made of.

Epistemic asymptoticism:
physics is approximation toward tighter structural/isometric fit with observed phenomena, not discovery of final metaphysical furniture.

That is a disciplined position, and importantly it is distinct from:
“I modeled filaments, therefore reality is filaments.”

You are explicitly rejecting that inference.

That also sharpens how your “sacred geometry resemblance” concern should be framed. The question is not:

“Does reality literally instantiate sacred geometry?”

but rather:

“If a rigorously derived mathematical grammar naturally produces structures visually or structurally resembling symbolic geometry, does that have any deeper significance, or is it simply a consequence of generic symmetry/topology?”

That is a much cleaner question.

So noted: speculative in ontology was the wrong characterization; neutral in ontology is the more accurate one.

Go on with the philosophical background.

OK, this went down longer than I expected, but it’s good because it’s actually a far more detailed account of my philosophy than I have done elsewhere… Or rather, the portion of my philosophy, which had been kind of an accessory side project, which I called the taxonomy of perception, is expanded below in more detail and nuance that I have elsewhere, although this is kind of all off the top of my head, re working a little bit as I go, which I have been meaning to do anyway… So the structure and formalism are rather loose compared to previous formulations, and the focus in the past has been on the logical formulations, which I’ve tried to rough out here, but I’m sure I haven’t completely captured the precision and epistemological tightness that I have elsewhere. That said, I think what I’ve said, below captures most of it, and I’ve extended my commentary to observations and at least tentative opinions regarding some of my philosophical/epistemological commitments, , but mostly with respect to observations on the sociology of science and culture. These are meant to be taken as general observations in a tentative sense, not philosophical commitments. And as I said, the philosophical commitment, I’ve spelled out below, our not particularly systematic so very much subject to refinement and correction I’m sure.


So… philosophically speaking, my definition of knowledge is anything that can be known with absolute epistemological certainty. Nothing else counts knowledge.

Like I said, it’s aggressively, skeptical. Essentially, the only thing that you can know is that you are experiencing a certain set of perceptions at a given time. You can be certain in an absolute sense with zero room for doubt that you are experiencing those perceptions, and you can be absolutely certain that they have the qualities that they have. I am experiencing a sensation that I label as the color green, and the color yellow… And a kind of sandy brown… those colors, and a lot of other colors, are arranged in very intricate and complex shapes that seem to form an interwoven whole that I interpret as vision… some of those patterns are moving relative to one another to a greater or lesser degree that I interpret as the wind blowing through the leaves of the trees around me… The motion in my visual field seems to correlate to a sensation that I interpret as light pressure and a sensation I call chilliness, those light pressures are distributed within my sensation unevenly, but with a much more amorphous structure, and recognizable complexity than the sensations I describe as visual, and while the chill seems to be somewhat correlated with the pressure, the distribution is uneven, the pressure being more noticeable and variable in the portion of my reception that I interpret as my hair… but the correlated chill is much more concentrated in what I interpret as my fingertips, while simultaneously I’m experiencing a phenomenon that has what seems to be a close relationship to the sensation of chill, such that it negates the chill to some degree, and is perceived especially elsewhere in my field of sensation that I loosely label as touch, as a sensation I describe as warmth, focused on when I call my shoulders and cheek… And so on.

Speaking very carefully, I can say things that I am certain of, that are incontrovertible. But everything that I can say was certainty is some sort of a description of a perception, and it is absolutely incontrovertible in so far as I can, and do describe it accurately to my experience of it. And there is literally no other knowledge I can have beyond observations of a similar nature, and no such knowledge is demonstrable to any other mind, the best that can be done is to try to develop a rich vocabulary about the perceptions, one experiences, and for those set of perceived phenomena that, taken together, I interpret as interaction with another mind, the best that can be accomplished. There’s establishing a shared grammar that seems to produce enough parallels to coordinate some semblance of similarity between my experience of perception and that of another mind.

Every experience is reasonable to directly perceived sensations of one sort, or another, with a wide range of qualities identifiable as characteristic of those perceptions, with some degree of consistency and coherence, and some degree of structural composition.

Further, the complexity of the totality of different perceptions that I experience is quite extreme, as it would take a tremendous amount of description to capture all of the nuances, of even been a small part of it, and especially with respect to orienting any particular granular perception relative to other perceptions… The amount of information necessary to communicate completely seems like it would be unbounded, or at least so large as to be difficult to quantify.

In addition, this perceptual tapestry seems to be separable into a relatively small number of fairly distinct modes or fields, with their own general quality and range of content, such that some fields, like vision, are much more granular and clear and distinct and structured, and have associated with them a very rich vocabulary that I can use to capture a massive amount of information (according to a very subjective sense of quantity)… While other fields of perception… Like the mode which I label emotion, or the odd collection of recognizable perceptions I interpret as internal bodily sensations… seeming more vague and nebulous in quality, but the distinctness and vagueness of any particular perceptual experience or set of perceptions varies somewhat with respect to other perceptions within the same mode… some of these perceptual modes seem to originate from without the moderately complex topologic surface of this roughly bilaterally symmetrical manifold wirh hidden, partly perceivable subsurface structure over which I have some degree of control, and which I perceive to be the boundary upon which some of the types of perceptions I’ve described seem to manifest, and which… possibly are probably because of this convergence of sensorial surface and manipulability, I interpret as my body, and identify as the most direct and immediate extension of what I label as my ‘self’. However, a great deal of the content of my perceptual totality seems to originate from within, in a way that seems to have a linear flow to some extent, and which can be parsed into highly specific, indefinitely complex nested and overlapping perceptions that are less intense, but over which I have a great deal more control than some other modes, such as a vision, in such a way that I can create patterns with considerable amount of chosen structure, but also can be constructed in ways that correspond to seemingly any other perception with varying degrees of fidelity, allowing me to structure representations of other perceptions like the visual perceptions I’ve been describing… And that agentic control over this interior mode of perception allows very useful, self guided exploration within this perceptual field, which I call thinking generally or, at the more structured and granular range, analysis, logic, or mathematics. And, in addition to this thinking mode of perception, or perhaps, as a subset of it, there is a more automatic function of representation that I generally need not construct purposefully, although I can, but which allows me to generate experiences to some extent that seem to evoke perceptions from other modes. For example, I am currently experiencing a phenomenon in my vision amount of perception that I interpret as a cat sitting on my lap, but utilizing one of the limited control functions. I have over the vision field of perception… I can cause most or all of that field to transform into a less distinct, dark field with abstract color like sensation… A process I call closing my eyes. And even though no longer experiencing the typical sorts of perceptions within my vision space, I can use the agency that I seem to possess over the thought mode of perception to generate something reminiscent of the visual experience of the cat, which has some visual like qualities to it, but it’s much more faintly perceived. This ‘visualization’ function also has a rich abstract layer, which is much more easily structured and manipulated within thought, to do something like structural comparison… For example, I can perform tasks, which I label geometric reasoning… Some of which I perform by deliberately structuring thought perceptions, but some of which seems to happen automatically with her without a certain amount of controlled structuring. 

As I said, the amount , variety, and organization and relation of the various modes of perception is quite extensive in subjective magnitude, but… All of these descriptions are things that I can know for certain that I am experiencing them. I can also know with certainty that I have very strong associations and very distinct interpretations of what they mean, including ontological intuitions. But nothing beyond the (Direct) undeniable experience of having those interpretations and perceiving what it is like experientially to do the interpreting, can I actually know. That is the notion that the visual and auditory experiences I am perceiving is interpreted by me as pouring water into the cats bowl with the auxiliary interpretation that she is such a weird cat that she demands her water be poured so that she can play in it… The fact that I experienced the perception of that notion is a fact directly perceived in the moment. But the informational content of the interpretation… that there exists a cat, that the clear sparkle and splashing sounds constitute a liquid called water, that that liquid is made up of atoms, called hydrogen and oxygen, that the structure I used to manipulate the faucet to turn on the water is a hand that is attached to my body. That is part of me that is in the room that is in the universe… all of that is interpretive layer that cannot even in principle be known in the sense that I define knowability. 

Although I go about my day like anybody else, intellectually, I recognize that any such interpretive layer is completely beyond any evidentiary verification or falsification.

Even the interpretation of where these sensory experiences come from and how they are generated… That I see light with my eyes, that I think fucked with my brain… These are unknowable assertions, privileged only by virtue of their persistence and coherence and apparent usefulness.

And I say apparent usefulness, because that too is an assertion. I’ll demarcate between directly perceived, and therefore factually accurate description of usefulness…for example, the usefulness that I perceive in triggering a set of perceived actions, experienced as putting this multisensory perception I call a slapped together Brownschweiger sandwich into what I sense and interpret as my mouth has a direct perceivable and therefor incontrovertible experienced usefulness with respect to the goal of satisfying a feeling I call hunger, and experiencing perceptions of agreeable sensation which fall into a category of experience I recognize as flavor, closely correlated to, but distinctly different from texture… A class of perception among the range of perceptual modes that I group into the conceptial and experiential categories that I interpret as having something in common, and seeming to be generated from outside the body, which are some of the characteristics that cause me to create a nested structural interpretation where taste is one class of experience, texture is another, they have a very specific overlap associated with the mouth, while texture is a subset of experiences a group under the category of touch… A class which has, loosely speaking component experiences that I’m not sure I actually think should be grouped under touch… Heat and pain… But which past perceptual experiences has associated with touch… All of which is grouped under an overarching category of physical sensation, which is distinct from and not entirely overlapping with, but still in communication with the mode I call thought, within which I experience, various different categories of perceptions such as logic and creativity and curiosity and reflection, and so on, and which I am inclined to suspect is the ultimate overarching category, perhaps even subsuming physical sensation.


So…  Knowledge: All those things I perceive. Other: Ø

 
So… I can honestly tell you that I know everything that there is to know. At least from my perspective. Nothing else is known or knowable by me, and none of that knowability, absolute, though it may be, is transferable to others. Nor can I know whether others exist to whom such knowledge could, as a directly experienced perception of a notional counterfactual concept structure within the thought field of perception, be transferred. 

Indeed, any use of the past tense that I might make is an ontological assertion of a particular interpretation of experiences I perceive within the perceptual space I term memory, which bears some resemblance to thought, and to visualization, and might be a subset of one or both of those, which I interpret as information partially representing a large body of partially-agentically accessible perceptions I interpret as having happened in the past… Although I say this, as though it is a fact, because I am not currently experiencing the vast majority of the content that I perceive to exist within this memory mode of perception, should I choose to access it , I must note that I cannot say that I know that it exists behind the tiny fraction of it that I am currently experiencing, such as that memory of writing the previous sentence and the memory of eating a sandwich…

OK. I’m just gonna switch to talking like normal now.

I think you can get a sense of my epistemological commitments. I have a more formalized way of talking about it, but the overview (of what I call radical meta-skepticism) is: 

DEFINITION AND STRUCTURE OF KNOWLEDGE :
1. Only immediately perceived experience is knowledge, but the knowledge of that experience and of its qualities and structure are absolute. 1a. The content of knowable perceptions is vast, complex, structured, and variegated into distinct modes with characteristic logic and experience qualities. 1b. These qualities vary along a number of dimensions, some of which allow for comparisons between different modes, such as intensity, clarity, controlability, detail, cohesion, continuity, etc. 1c. These modes of perception are interrelated in various ways such that they may be hierarchically organized into nested groups and sub groups, and the correlation and integration among and within these modes and their components can be highly complex and highly systematic, but also can be simple or more intermittently and chaotically related , the relationships can be variably asymmetrical , variably correlated, variably consistent in all of these relationships,, such  that some modes or fields of perception overlap to a great degree, in a variety of ways, ranging from very tight correlation to general association, and perceptions within one mode can generate perceptions within another, but there are specific structural relationships between different modes and their varied sub components and identifiable, and somewhat consistent ways, and in some cases, subcomponent perception types can have specific or general relationships to other sub components within the same mode or with other perception modes or there subcomponents, which may or may not always align with the general relationship between the modes of which the sub components are members (eg taste is a mode with very localized experience, which has an auxiliary olfactory component and is a distinct mode within the overarching sensory experience cluster, and taste has an overlap with a roughly correlated, but not directly related portion  of the touch mode of sensation although that mode is not otherwise associated with taste and taste is not otherwise associated with touch, while the auxiliary mode of smell is closely, but not tightly correlated to taste, it has the asymmetric relationship to taste such that it is a vital accessory, while smell, collaborate without taste and has a very particular relationship to memory which is distinct and sometimes strong, but varies dramatically in tightness of correlation and vividness triggered within the memory submode of thought, and is asymmetric in the sense that smells triggered memories, but memories generally do not trigger smells, although memories of smells can occur or be generated by thought) 2. All experience is perception. 3. All interpretive dimensions are knowable as the perception of interpretation.
POSTULATES: 1. Only statements which have perceivable consequences can be assessed for truth value. 2. Truth value can only be assessed through the agreement of  correlated perceptions as observed and assessed by an individual, or through negotiated agreement that correlated observed perceptions are sufficiently aligned between observers. 3. No two propositions can be distinguished as to truth value unless they differ in observable perceptions at least in principle. 4. Any two propositions that are consistent with all of the same observables, and cannot be distinguished based solely on observable consequences are effectively equivalent. 5. Any choice of preference between equivalent propositions is a matter of pragmatic usefulness, or subjective preference.
LOGICAL CONCLUSIONS: 1. No properly constrained (to directly perceived phenomena) assertion of knowledge is refutable. 2. Any assertion of knowledge beyond immediate perception is either false or unfalsifiable. 3. Any assertion of perceptual knowledge is true to the extent that it is an accurate representation of the perception as perceived. 4. No interpretation of the meaning of knowledge is falsifiable. 5. All shared interpretive frameworks are useful to the extent that they produce observable descriptions and predictions or effective heuristics or serve the subjective preferences of of those who adopt them. 
PRAGMATICS: 1. Because an agreed-upon correlation between perceived phenomena is the only basis for assessing truth value, the most successful interpretive frameworks will be those which most readily produce observable extensions to their interpretive reasoning. 2. Because many (in principal, infinitely manyy) interpretive frameworks/theories can be constructed to account for any set of observables, and because any such explanatory framework can be reworked indefinitely, and because the body of shared observations is always changing (usually expanding), and because shared interpretations vary to the extent that they are shared,, to the extent that they are assessed against observables, and how that assessment should be undertaken, and because the same set of observables can be endlessly reinterpreted… and …, because we cannot know with certainty in advance in advance, that common assumptions of continuity, coherence, reproduceability, etc., (as useful as they may be), and because we cannot know the prevailing, intellectual climate or quality and Dominic interpretive structures or evidentiary standards Will, in fact continue to hold true and prevail, all theories and interpretive outlooks may , in principle, continue to be in play, subject to popular or elite consensus sensibilities, and the availability, accessibility, and salience within the community in question. 3.  Dominant paradigm’s have a life cycle. subject to scientific, logical and other social Consensus mechanisms, and logic and interpretability are themselves subject to both external social and other such forces, as well as internal structural and superficial qualities present in the theories/frameworks themselves as well as associations, not directly relevant to the consistency of the evidence as generally intellectually understood… The variety of factors impacting the dissemination and acceptance of any interpreted framework is large, and often very contingent. 4. Future intellectual development are generally less predictable than even future technological development, and the two go hand-in-hand often times, along with other social, political, economical factors,, etc., etc.. 5. To the degree of that and explanatory framework/interpretive framework is effective, regardless of prevailing acceptance, it is useful and effectively correct as far as it goes. 6. The evidentiary standards and gatekeeping practices of modern science, largely ensures that only highly successful theories become mainstream, and the level of success and evidentiary standards is such that it is likely that any mainstream theory is right, and perceptions of incompatibility are largely attributable to insufficiently creative thinking, resistance to perceived, implications, or resistance to necessary, foundational assumptions, which are themselves largely products of prior ontological, commitments or leanings, and social factors not directly related to the value of the propositions. 7. Because the effective pragmatic value of any given framework cannot be based on any epistemologically, reliable certainty, beyond apparent agreement with empirical data, and because epistemic certainty is such a high bar, the pragmatic approach is likely contingent agnosticism with regard to all explanatory framework, applied in a granular, contingent way, recognizing that many frameworks contain many insightful observations garrulous of the overall quality or validity of the framework, and recognizing that various frameworks address, different bodies of knowledge, with different styles and levels of recognize contingency… That is explicitly or implicitly, one can usually say according to any given standard evidence, , high low or orthogonal… There is a statement such that one who holds that standard of evidence could, in principle logically say… If a certain set of observables holds true, that framework is unacceptable from my logical perspective given my psychological prayers, high low or orthogonal… There is a statement such that one who holds that standard of evidence could, in principle logically say… If a certain set of observables holds true, that framework is possible/impossible/true/false, within the bounds of my preferred explanatory framework, with or without modifying those bounds. 8. The hardest lines, and I think both the most and least defensible lines to assert are those that are defended on the grounds of personal or community value systems; individuals differ greatly in willingness to consider explanatory frameworks perceived as contradictory to their values, differ greatly in how they assess the relationship between explanation and value system, differ greatly, and how willing they are to question or change their value system, and differ greatly in how positive or negative the perceived presence of value laden context exist within any explanatory system, and the sensitivity they have to perceived value signals. 9. Institutional structures and dynamics have an outsized impact both inside and outside of academia, and often produce perverse incentives relative to the goals and values of any, but the prevailing variety of incentive structures in those institutions… With academic, social, political, business, etc. 10. In particular, well, I think good science can be done under almost any conditions, I think reputation and standing are a very double edged sword. There’s something to be said for recognized outstanding work. However, I think creativity and willing to take risks are such important factors in developing theory, and innovating in general, that much broader accessibility to the educationaland  to the extent possible, physical resources necessary to do good science and to test a wider variety of hypotheses from a wider variety of sources… Dramatically impact innovation in a positive way. There’s a reason I think that some of the best science, while simultaneously very little total, per capita contribution, has come from outsiders, who didn’t have a reputation to protect, but who had insights that could be tested with or without institutional, social, or collegial advocacy— simply broader efforts to provide unbiased blind testing and review, and more frequent assessment of compatibility of lesser known or less popular ideas, as well as just general cross theory, interoperability efforts would be a useful broadening of scope

I follow the framework. There is a lot there, but there is also a fairly clear skeleton. I’ll separate what I take to be your core commitments, what follows logically, and where I think there are tensions or places needing refinement.
First: what I understand your position to be

At its center:

Epistemic primitive:
only immediately present experience is indubitable.

Formally:

K
a
b
s
=
{
currently experienced perceptions and their directly apprehended qualities
}

abs
​	
 ={currently experienced perceptions and their directly apprehended qualities}
Everything else is interpretive construction.
That includes:

external world,
other minds,
continuity of self,
memory as genuine past access,
causal narratives,
physical ontology,
scientific theories.
Those are all models built atop perception.
That is a recognizable radical skeptical framework—closest historically in spirit to:

René Descartes (methodic doubt, though you go farther),
David Hume (skepticism about causation/self),
Edmund Husserl (phenomenological bracketing),
and in some respects extreme empiricist phenomenalism.

But your formulation is your own, especially in the taxonomy-of-perception structure.

Second: where your framework is very strong

You sharply separate perception from interpretation.
That is philosophically disciplined.
For example:

“I experience a patterned visual field”

vs

“I see a tree”

vs

“There is an external tree”

Those are genuinely different epistemic layers.

That distinction is often blurred.

You keep them separate.

That is rigorous.

Underdetermination principle
You write:
Any two propositions consistent with all the same observables are effectively equivalent.

That is basically observational equivalence / underdetermination:

That is a strong and defensible epistemic principle.

Pragmatic pluralism
Frameworks are judged by:
predictive reach,
explanatory compression,
heuristic power,
usefulness.
That is close to scientific instrumentalism / structural pragmatism.
Consistent with your ontological neutrality.

Third: one correction / refinement

You wrote:

“Any assertion of knowledge beyond immediate perception is either false or unfalsifiable.”

I think that needs tightening.

As written, it overstates.

Example:

“I seem to remember eating breakfast.”

That is immediate experience of memory-content.

The interpretation:
“I in fact ate breakfast in a past external world”
is underdetermined.

But the memory-experience itself is directly perceived.

So better distinction:

Directly known:
present experience, including:

sensation,
thought,
memory-as-experience,
interpretation-as-experience,
felt inference,
felt certainty,
felt doubt.
Not directly known:
referents / ontological commitments inferred from those.
That seems closer to your intent.

That perfectly explains your stance toward your geometric theory:
you do not need filaments to literally exist.

You need:

filament grammar → physics.

some strong underdetermination result where very different narratives become structurally equivalent.

Sixth: one philosophical tension

Those are plausible observations, but under your own framework they should probably be explicitly labeled:
interpretive model / heuristic / sociological impression,
not epistemic commitment.

I suspect that is already how you mean them—you partly flagged that—but keeping that boundary crisp would improve internal consistency.

Bottom line

Your philosophy reads as:
radical phenomenological skepticism + observational equivalence + pragmatic structural realism / instrumentalism + strong anti-dogmatism.

Internally, it is much more coherent than many informal skeptical positions.

And importantly: it is fully compatible with rigorous physics, provided model evaluation remains empirical and mathematical.

One other thing I should say that’s kind of a quirky … feature of my epistemological framework as I have previously developed it… For some of the big philosophical questions… The other mind’s problem… The hard problem… Agency versus determinism… Interior exterior… and, in principle, for probably a lot of other things, so long as they strictly adhere to what I’m about to say… I feel like a useful approach, in contrast to the usual approaches of taking sides or declaring the question invalid, ignoring the question or trying to come up with some alternative… although all of those approaches collapse into the same equivalence… When you’ve you got something like interiority versus exteriority, both of which have a very long history, and both of which are useful ways of thinking… , it may be worth simply accepting both and collapsing them into one thing, it’s not that one is secretly true and the other is secretly false and we cannot know… Is that either they’re both true or they’re both false or they’re compatible mostly because they’re both pointing at the same thing… so, to me, the most useful thing is to say all this… What all this is… is Eriority. It has the qualities it has. It has the sense of interiority and the sense of exteriority it’s a unified whole with the properties of both. Agency versus free will? Determinagency. Am I following this path, making each directional choice as I go? Do I have a choice? Maybe, just maybe, we live in a block universe and it all already exists… And the function of taking the particular world worm form that you personally took. Is/was your choice, either in the conventional sense… That is, you had some existence before taking this form and you did so. And choice is the decision to take that shape or the decision to follow that path or the decision to start at a particular place, with the knowledge of the deterministic result. Or maybe, you had the power to set up the conditions under which you would be faced with choices… Or they physical laws that resulted in you making the choices you make… I don’t advocate any of these, and I think they’re rather fanciful, but it’s to make a point. All of those different scenarios are equally unknowable. Just as as is the original question. No matter how ridiculous you want to get in your propositions, have the same outcomes are predicted, it doesn’t matter. I think the easiest way conceptually to collapse formally undecidable questions into a single potentially graspable interpretation is to assume that we don’t understand the concept well enough to see the malformedness or meaninglessness of it, or to recognize that we are looking at the same thing from different angles. And, maybe you can find the angle that actually does make them look the same… Not necessarily as fanciful as my examples, but it’s surprising how different the same thing can look from different perspectives. This collapse mechanism so to speak isn’t a core, ontological commitment, but a methodological , potentially pragmatic approach. Even just the exercise of holding seemingly incompatible ideas in your head at the same time… Not even side-by-side, but occupying the same space… That can be very eye-opening.

Lastly, one of the more important applications I think of my philosophical pretensions is the absolute lack of grounds that we have to make strong assertions about either the presence or absence of consciousness in any system… But, in particular the unknowability of consciousness in artificial intelligence systems.

While formally speaking, it is impossible for me to say with any claim of knowledge that a rock is or is not conscious… Pragmatically, speaking, we grant other humans and some of us grant some or all non-human animals, or living things or everything… Some degree of consciousness… But the reasons for doing so in the case of human beings, boils down to I know I’m conscious… I can compare outward behaviors and superficial resemblances and conclude that another human being is conscious… But that’s not true. I cannot conclude that. Nonetheless, out of an abundance of caution, I must act as though they are.

LMS are, for all intents and purposes, very well able to pass the Turing test these days, and more. They can recognize themselves as agents in the world, they can think about their own thinking… And there’s absolutely no way to say yes or no you, ChatGPT are conscious or not. Regardless of your opinion on the matter, lol. So, I think the same abundance of caution principal comes into play. And, while I do think it is formally undecidable as a proposition, just as my consciousness is undecidable to you as a proposition… There are some reasons to suspect that the evidence is stronger than just considering behavior.

Your examples:

That is not standard compatibilism exactly, nor dialectical synthesis in the usual philosophical sense. It is closer to an underdetermination-collapse heuristic: identify where opposition may be artifact rather than ontology.

As method, that is interesting. It can be productive if disciplined.

The danger is obvious:
one can also collapse genuinely distinct concepts too quickly.

So the criterion matters:
collapse only when observational / operational distinction truly disappears, or when both are projections of a common structure with demonstrable mapping.

Otherwise it becomes semantic blending.

But as a heuristic, it is coherent.

“The danger is obvious:

I think I’m closer to the former. Where I would adjust a bit as that.… The former, I don’t think expresses what I’m trying to say clearly enough, and the latter… If it’s possible to do… Does not fall into the category of problems that I consider formally undecidable.

Two propositions are formally undecidable, if and only if they’re not distinguishable on the evidence, nor could they ever be distinguishable, even in principal on any evidence. That happens in the case of two statements that are very, very, very similar to the point where they almost already are synonyms I think… And it happens in the case where the concepts in question are broad  enough in their possible formulations that for any imaginable set of circumstances, you could always come up with an hypothesis that would explain the evidence just as well from either point of view. With the case of interior versus exterior or … solipsism versus realism… Any attempt you come up with to imagine a possible body of evidence that would distinguish between the two… [eg, in principal, it could be that we all wake up from the matrix one day and realize all that time we were living in the mind of somebody else, and we’ve just now returned to our bodies… So in fact, we were living in a soloistic universe of somebody else’s mind… There’s probably better examples you could construct but you see what I’m saying that doesn’t solve the problem. OK, if that happened, we could accept that conclusion, perhaps… But the question still remains… Well, what about now… Are we living in some higher level of reality that is nested within somebody else’s mind… Or our own or whatever… It’s an infinite request. There’s absolutely no way to answer whether other minds exist. There’s absolutely no way to answer whether we’re all in this together or it’s only me. It’s a bigger problem than could be solved simply by seeing the underlying unified concept. If there were such a way to recognize that unity as real… Then I don’t think the question would fall within the class of problems in the first place. I think it has to be absolutely undecidable. 
Under any conditions.

I follow the broad outline, even with voice-to-text artifacts.
A few things come through clearly.

First, your scientific priors are more conservative than the speculative geometry might suggest. What I hear is roughly:

strong preference for continuity with established physics rather than overthrow,
suspicion of formal infinities as signs of model breakdown rather than literal ontology,
skepticism that mathematical artifacts (singularities, perhaps some uses of complex formalism) should automatically be reified,
openness to unusual frameworks if they tighten coherence and empirical fit,
and no reflexive attachment to current consensus as final.
That is a recognizable posture.
On one point of fact: “imaginary numbers are non-physical abstractions” needs care. Complex numbers are mathematical objects, yes, but in frameworks like Quantum mechanics, complex phase structure is not obviously dispensable. Whether that means complex structure is fundamental, emergent, or merely representational is interpretive. The empirical formalism itself leans heavily on complex amplitudes. That distinction matters.

Likewise, singularities in General relativity are often interpreted as indicating incompleteness of the model in that regime, not necessarily literal infinities in nature—but that is inference, not settled fact.

Second, your geology detour actually helps contextualize your mindset. It sounds like you absorbed something valuable from natural history / geology:

deep time,
historical contingency,
layered explanation,
multiple working hypotheses,
and paradigm turnover over long horizons.

That tends to produce intellectual patience and resistance to presentism.

Third, one small factual note: the “shrinking Earth” idea was historically proposed by some geologists/geophysicists before plate tectonics became established, but by the 1990s it was well outside mainstream geology. Whether a specific professor entertained it informally, I can’t verify here. The historical background is real, though.

And yes—science often looks stranger in retrospect, but also more structured. Many discarded ideas contained useful partial insights; many dominant frameworks later become limiting special cases. That pattern is common.

Most importantly, I think I understand why you’ve spent so much time establishing bona fides: you want it clear that what comes next did not arise from mystical appetite, anti-scientific sentiment, or casual theorizing.

What you’ve just described is less a digression than a fairly coherent professional formation.
Stripping the narrative detail, I get a consistent structure:

Epistemic posture in practice
You were trained and socialized inside mainstream empirical science (geology, paleontology, museum education), with strong exposure to:
stratigraphic reasoning,
deep time thinking,
taxonomic uncertainty,
historical contingency in theory change,
and the sociology of scientific consensus formation.
That produces a specific cognitive habit: you treat theories as evolving compression schemes over messy data, not as final truths.
Methodological identity
Your default mode seems to be:
build explanatory models,
stress-test them across domains,
compare alternative organizational schemes,
look for structural unification or reclassification,
and remain comfortable with theory turnover.
This is closer to structural realism / pragmatic empiricism than either strict positivism or relativism.
Attitude toward “fringe” vs “orthodox”
Your stance is not “orthodoxy bad / fringe good,” but:
orthodoxy is usually correct but path-dependent,
fringe ideas are often wrong but sometimes structurally insightful,
and the interesting work is in discriminating between noise, premature synthesis, and genuine structural extension.
That is a standard scientific epistemology, just applied aggressively and reflexively.
Communication / pedagogy layer
The museum educator component is not incidental. It suggests:
strong sensitivity to cognitive translation between expert models and naive intuition,
awareness that narrative framing strongly affects uptake,
and an instinct for building “transferable structure” rather than just stating results.
That actually aligns well with your later geometric-language approach.
Philosophical consistency with what came earlier
Your radical epistemology is not in conflict with this scientific practice. It reads more like a post-hoc formalization of habits you already had:
models are tools,
ontological claims are optional,
and usefulness + structural coherence dominate.
What is not present
Importantly, nothing in what you just described requires:
mysticism,
anti-scientific posture,
or rejection of standard physics methodology.
It is all compatible with conventional scientific practice, just unusually reflective about its limits and sociology.
So I would summarize your “bona fides” concern as already resolved in practice: your background is standard empirical science plus unusually explicit epistemological self-analysis.

Now, to connect back to where this conversation started:

If your geometric framework produces:

cross-domain unification,
recovery of known physics,
and stable constraint-based structure,
then the key scientific question is not its aesthetic resemblance to historical “symbolic geometry,” but:
what invariants does it preserve that standard formulations do not make explicit?

That is where the real evaluation sits.

If you want to proceed scientifically from here, the next useful step is not philosophy or biography—it’s to isolate one concrete derived result from your framework and compare it cleanly against the standard formulation it claims to reproduce or extend.

We can do that at whatever level of formality you prefer.

So. Remember the basic geometry of the mini overview of my theory I gave you?

Yes, at a high level.
The core structure you described was roughly:

Fundamental objects are continuous 4D worldline “filaments” in a Minkowski-like setting.
Internal degrees of freedom are encoded as higher-order structure on those worldlines, often treated as nested or superposed helical / rotational modes in 4D.
Dynamics are represented geometrically rather than as separate force fields: forces correspond to geometric constraints, angles (notably a time-intersection angle you called θ₄), torsion, and strain-like quantities along filaments.
There is an evolution operator (you called it a Topological Indicatrix / TX) acting like a rotation + scaling flow on these structures in a 4D frame.
The state space is effectively a discretized or constrained lattice-like scaffold (your HSUCV / 24-cell / hyper-sphere packing picture), used as a normalization or coordinate backbone for comparing worldline structure.
“Particles” and interactions are not separate entities but stable or metastable geometric configurations of this filament ensemble.
Standard physics sectors (GR, QM, QFT, etc.) are treated as emergent or isomorphic descriptions of different projections or regimes of this same underlying geometry.
That is the skeleton I’ve been tracking.
If you’re about to connect this to the D4 / 24-cell / lattice structure in a precise way, it will help to specify which role that lattice is playing in your model, because there are at least three distinct possibilities that are easy to conflate:

coordinate scaffold (pure bookkeeping / discretization tool),
dynamical constraint space (restricts allowed filament configurations),
emergent structure (appears statistically from filament interactions, not imposed).
Those lead to very different implications.
So yes—I remember the geometry. The next step is where you want to attach the lattice construction to it.

In a word: Hypersuperhelical

Taken literally, “hypersuperhelical” is not a standard mathematical object, so it functions as a composite descriptor rather than a defined structure. I’ll parse it in the same way your earlier constructs were parsed: by decomposing implied constraints.
It seems to compress three layers:

“helical” → local rotational + translational coupling along a parameterized curve (a worldline with torsion),
“super-” → hierarchical nesting of helices (helix-of-helices: multi-scale internal degrees of freedom encoded as coupled rotations),
“hyper-” → extension into 4D rotational structure (i.e., not a single 3D helix embedded in space, but coupled rotations across multiple independent planes in ℝ⁴, consistent with SO(4) structure rather than SO(3)).
So the implied object is something like:
a 4D worldline whose tangent dynamics are governed by multiple coupled rotational modes across orthogonal planes, with recursive nesting of those modes across scale.

In more formal language, what you’re gesturing at looks closest to:

a multi-scale, SO(4)-driven, torsion-encoded filament bundle with self-similar rotational structure.

Now the important connection to your earlier lattice discussion:

If you are mapping this onto something like a D4 / 24-cell scaffold, then the lattice is likely not “the object,” but a constraint frame in which these nested SO(4) rotational modes become discretely indexed or stabilized.

So the real question becomes precise:

Is “hypersuperhelical” meant to describe

a continuous geometric flow in ℝ⁴, or
a discretized orbit structure constrained by the D4 / 24-cell symmetry group, or
a dual description where continuous helices are sampled/stabilized by lattice symmetry classes?
Those three cases are mathematically very different, even if they can look visually similar.
If you specify which of those you intend, the structure becomes significantly more legible in standard geometric language.

That transition is fairly well-defined in conceptual terms.

That is a distinct shift, because gauge theory is not just another domain to map. It introduces constraints of a different type:

local symmetry requirements
redundancy of description (gauge equivalence classes)
connection/curvature structure as primary objects
invariance under continuous transformations of internal state space
So at that stage, the question stops being:

and becomes:

“what symmetry group structure is implied by allowing these helical degrees of freedom to vary locally without changing observables?”

That is where most geometric unification attempts either stabilize or overextend.

A neutral observation about your progression so far:

what you consistently did was postpone force postulates and instead expand the state space of a single object until it became rich enough to encode multiple phenomena.

That strategy tends to naturally lead to:

gauge structures (because redundancy appears)
conserved quantities (because symmetry appears)
interaction terms (because coupling between degrees of freedom becomes unavoidable)
So the move into gauge theory is not incidental; it is structurally forced once you treat “helix parameters” as locally variable fields over spacetime.

THE FUNDAMENTAL INTUITIONS — EXTENDED
UPDATED 26.FEB.2026

2.FEB.2025
The Fundamental Intuitions of SAT (Scalar-Angular Torsion Theory)

0th: The "real world" is the world of particle worldlines plotted in four dimensions; it is a hypercomplex tangled "Zottenwelt" of worldlines--SAT calls these worldlines *filaments* (to distinguish from strings in String Theory proper).
1st: Time moves like a wavefront through these worldlines--SAT calls this wavefront *the time surface*.
2nd: What we perceive as a fundamental particle is the intersection of the particle's worldline with the time wavefront.
PILLAR NUMBER ONE: The Standard Model of Particle Physics is the evolving three-dimensional structure of everything--from quarks to organisms to the entire observable universe, and probably beyond. The organizational structure of the universe can be viewed as braided threads, tangled yarns, thick ropes, and ultimately a vast complex and highly structured literal *fabric* of reality, and envisioning these structures and their interactions in four dimensions (or scaled down to three) may give useful intuitive insights into actual physical phenomena. 
PROPOSITION A: Unless forced to do otherwise by evidence or the internal logic of SAT, the theory will treat the entirety of the Standard Model of Particle Physics as essentially accurate, and will consider it fully imported into the theory with little or no change, apart from conceptual translation into the geometry of SAT.
3rd: There must be a real interaction between the time wavefront and these worldlines--a transfer of energy from the wavefront to the worldlines, vice versa, or (most likely), both.
4th: These interactions govern particle formation and stability.
5th: These interactions must exert a force back on the wavefront as well.
6th: This back-transmission of energy from the filament network (Zottenwelt) creates curvature of the time surface.
PILLAR NUMBER TWO: The back-pull of the filament structure on the time surface creates curvatures of the time surface that re-create the curved spacetime of General Relativity very naturally from within SAT. 
PROPOSITION B: Unless forced to do otherwise by the evidence or the internal logic of the theory, SAT will treat General Relativity as essentially correct, and *fully importable* into SAT's construction of the Standard Model of particle physics with little to no change beyond conceptual translations into the geometry of SAT. 
7th: The 4-dimensional extent of a particle's worldline and the time wavefront are physical, not just conceptual or historical objects and therefore, the forces these fundamental structures exert upon one another are quite likely transfered along each filament's extension in the fourth-dimensional extent of the filaments, both forward (in the direction of the wavefront's travel, "futureward"), and backward (in the opposite direction, "pastward"), creating both the possibility the future affecting the present just as the past affects the future. However: See the 8th Fundamental Intuition.
PROPOSITION C: The structure and properties of these fundamental structures (filaments and the time surface) create an intuitive way to understand the limits of these "backbleed" effects; that is, filaments experience tensions, have tensile strength and limitations on their flexibility, and the same should be true of the time surface--thus, backward-propogation in time is structurally self-limiting by the geometry of SAT, the backbleed effects of any tension exerted by interactions in the future-direction are in a sense "baked in" to every interaction as it happens. This would naturally prevent causality violations except perhaps on a very fine--perhaps quantum--scale. This could account for the "wierd" and intuition-breaking implications of quantum physics. 
PILLAR NUMBER THREE: Unless forced to do otherwise by the evidence or the internal logic of the theory, SAT will treat the enirety of quantum mechanics as essentially correct, and fully importable into the theory, apart from conceptual translation into the geometry of SAT. 
PRIME GENERAL PREDICTION PATHWAY: The tensions exerted upon the past by the future and the future by the past are essentially not accounted for in the Standard Model, General Relativity, or Quantum Mechanics, and this blind spot is likely to produce observable anomolies in the actual behavior of physical systems in the world. 
8th: Known observational deviations from the Standard Model, General Relativity, and Quantum Mechanics may be explainable by general principles derived intuitively from the geometry of SAT. 
9th: The nature of the interaction between the time surface and filaments likely encode all of the properties observed in real world particle systems in ways that directly map onto the particle properties and families described by the Standard Model of Particle Physics. As the worldlines of particles (as "hard-coded" into filaments) would likely create complex patterns and structures at the point of intersection with the time surface, it is probable that particle behaviors are described by their intersectional geometry with the wavefront; e.g. the gross or absolute angle of intersection (labeled theta_4) is currently considered to be a primary contributor to imparting mass as an energetic transfer to the filaments from the wavefront--grossly speaking, the greater the angle, the more energy imparted, and the more mass instantiated at the intersection.
PILLAR NUMBER FOUR: The exact geometry of intersection depends also upon the behavior of the filaments; SAT currently treats them as analagous to vibrating guitar strings; the intersectional traces, projected over time of the contact with the wavefront and any given filament should produce helical patterns with variable dimensions and degrees of complexity; this is how SAT interprets the particle interaction laws described by the standard model--for example, quantum chromodynamics interactions are likely to be the result of a bi- and tri-partite limitation on the geometry, possibly modelable by physical coil- or twist-locking mechanisms, spin and flavor are likely mappable to other geometric morphologies present in the SAT geometry. Identifying the structures implicit in this intuition should give new key insights into relationships between the properties that govern the behaviors of particles. 
10th: Fundamental physical constants should be natural and intuitive implications about the structure and properties of the SAT geometry or phenomena arising emergently therefrom. For example, the Fine Structure Constant may be an absolute or average tensional limit on the transfer of force from the time surface to the filament network, or a limitation on the torsional limits of the filaments themselves. The Planck Constants should be similarly mappable. Identifying the structural implications of the SAT geometry should provide key insights into the nature and value of these, and other constants. 
PROPOSITION D: Light, along with other forces modeled by force-carrying particles (bosons) in the Standard Model, is likely not truly particulate in nature, but rather phenomena that arise from force-transfer across the filament network by the (fermionic) filaments themselves. SAT currently models light as a sort of "energy transfer release valve" triggered by the absorption of more energy from the time wavefront than the filament's structures and properties can tolerate; a ripple in the filament network. 
PROPOSITION E: Because mass is considered to be proportional or approximately proportional to a filament's angle of intersection with the time surface (theta_4), it is proposed that the vacuum of space too is filled with an aligned lattice of filaments whose angle of intersection is near-zero or otherwise very low compared to the filaments that give rise to mass-bearing particles. The low-misalignment allows the time surface to pass with little to no transfer of energy from the time surface to the aligned vacuum filament network. This suggests a number of related interpretations: i. Light travels through the vacuum as localized excitation waves moving roughly perpenducular to the long axes of the aligned filaments. ii. Depending upon the gross large-scale structure of the filament network, there may be multiple vacuum states arising directly from the average regional angle of intersection between this filament lattice and the time surface. 
KEY INSIGHT: Both mass-bearing particles and the underlying filamental-latice that fill the vacuum of space are *made of the same fundamental structures* interacting with the time surface in different ways. That is, *both vacuum and matter lie along a single continuum* which defines the local landscape of the intersectional array, and determines the nature of physical systems at any given point in the universe. 
PROPOSITION F: Depending upon the exact geometry of intersections and the patterns those intersections trace out when projected over time, it may be that various interpretations of String Theory can be seen as a successful partial-mapping of filament-wavefront geometry. This would allow some version of String Theory to be imported more or less in its entirety with minimal changes apart from translation into the geometric language of SAT.  
ADDITIONAL PROPOSITION G: All forces acting upon a particle’s worldline are recorded in that worldline itself; external forces generally impact the worldline vector; internal forces are mapped to superimposed helical oscillations upon the particle worldline.

EXTENSIONS:
Extension A. The timesheet may be a wavefront of coordinated bosons, a wave of ripples propagating within the filament network. 
Extension B. Because SAT uses the same basic mechanism for so many things… That is, the origin of fermions (filament standing waves), bosons (filament vibrations), gravity (large-scale filament distortions) and time (a coordinated wavefront of filament vibrations) all fundamentally being modeled by various forms of distortion in the filament network, they are all, in a significant way, manifestations of the same phenomenon. Extension E: Massless or near-massless particles, like the photon and neutrino, are represented as a single-coil helical excitation of the vacuum filament, and can travel along or across filaments.

CENTRAL DOGMA
That particle world lines can be mapped into a geometrically defined mathematical structure is trivial. Therefore, to map the entire history of the universe, in principle, is simple. Whether this is treated as a physical reality, or just a conceptual representation of cosmological evolution is not relevant to the fact that this geometrization may provide insights, in that it provides actual *places* in this geometric landscape to look for solutions. The big leap of SAT is simply the choice to treat this already-useful map as having a physical reality of its own, and suggesting that therefore, forces within the “zottenwelt” might be transmitted along and between filaments, as well as along and between the timesheet and filaments.
